# Counterexample and consistency audit

No genuine counterexample to the whole-space decaying Euler conjecture has been established. The report handoff separates exact profiles, singular or nondecaying examples, and kinematic countertests. The detailed candidate ledger and equation checks follow the current result ledger below.

LIT397: PROVED at CONFIRMED A3223-A3224; broader scope OPEN A3225. For real C1 solenoidal fields with E(R)/(LR^beta)->1, L>0 and0<beta<1, an additional finite logarithmic gradient coefficient M gives the negative-spherical rate at most(M-H)/(2-beta), about every fixed center. The coefficient1/(2-beta) is optimal for a bound required to hold for every admissible gradient excess delta=M-H; sharpness follows as delta tends to zero. The best beta-uniform coefficient is1. Optimality at a prescribed positive delta remains OPEN. Total radial derivatives improve the modal comparison, with an exact integer positivity certificate and velocity-only cutoff errors for all angular degrees. The positive-source bound also improves, without an optimality claim. No automatic finite M, eventual sign, pointwise negative-part bound or unrestricted Euler nonexistence follows.

LIT396: PROVED at CONFIRMED A3215-A3216; broader scope OPEN A3217. The exact modal constant at each fixed exponent is C_beta=max(1,(2b_2+t^2)/(2(2-beta)b_2)), with b_2=(k^2+2)/2+2beta/(k^2+2). A rank-one identity proves the degree-one value; an exact positive determinant proves Q_pol+Z_pol>=0 for every real lambda>=6. The high-degree jet limit makes1 necessary. The resulting negative spherical coefficient is at most C_beta(M-H), and C_beta<16/13 for each0<beta<1. Finite M remains additional; no optimal global coefficient or Euler nonexistence is claimed.

LIT395: PROVED at CONFIRMED A3207/A3209, with A103 compatibility CONFIRMED A3210; literal arXiv v2 displays REFUTED A3208; broader scope OPEN A3211. The corrected projected flux is (|v_tilde|^2-|h|^2)v+2p_0 v_tilde and the bulk is2(v tensor v):Dh. For classical local Euler, uniform local L2, finite critical cubic supremum and vanishing critical cubic liminf exclude a terminal atom. Three exact smooth Euler examples refute only the printed formulas and fixed-ball chain; they fail target decay. The separate critical-profile pure-point argument retains its finite-energy, Type I and direct-trace premises. The journal main text and complete weak compactness argument remain unaudited.

LIT394: PROVED at A3203-A3204 CONFIRMED; broader scope OPEN A3205. For the smooth radial oscillation family, arbitrary periodic mean-zero sources give the exact negative-spherical-source envelope F_beta(delta)=(sqrt(C_s^2+4C_s delta/(2-beta))-C_s)/2. Cauchy-Schwarz proves the upper bound; smooth mollification and exact variance restoration approach it, and no smooth member attains it. All-center O(R^-1) energy, derivative decay and nonzero Euler residual curl are retained. The envelope has slope1/(2-beta) at zero, so every universal kinematic linear coefficient must be at least1/(2-beta), or at least1 if uniform in beta. The family envelope is not asserted universal over all fields or actual profiles.

LIT393: PROVED at A3194; global optimum OPEN A3196. The exact modal inequality Q_pol+(16/13)Z_pol>=0 replaces the earlier constant4, uniformly in beta and every angular degree. A degree-one square completion and determinant prove the low mode; elementary uniform Schur bounds cover all higher modes. The jet(19,3,-13) as beta tends to1 makes16/13 the optimal uniform modal constant. The resulting logarithmic negative-spherical-source coefficient is at most min(M,(16/13)(M-H)); the positive counterpart has the corresponding improved upper bound. The finite-gradient premise remains additional, and optimality of the global logarithmic constant is not claimed.

LIT392: PROVED in A3197 and A3199; invalid inferences REFUTED in A3198 and A3200; broader conclusions OPEN A3201. An audit of Xue arXiv:1408.6619v2 gives smooth periodic solenoidal fields with pointwise time maximum comparable to bracket(y)^epsilon but maximum ball energy O(R^(N-1)), refuting the growth-only inferences used in (5.2) and (5.16). Their full periodic Euler residual has nonzero curl, so they are not Euler counterexamples. The pressure lemma uses finite physical energy. Theorem 1.2(2) must retain its stated zero affine pressure coefficient; a free-affine reading admits constant Euler profiles and is false. A critical-energy lemma repairs the fixed-time estimate in Theorem 1.1(2) from its spacetime L2 and normalized-pressure premises. The critical K3 weight is one; below criticality it can be discarded on exterior radii for a weaker bound. The full normalized theorem and annular bootstrap have not been independently certified.

LIT391: PROVED at A3186 CONFIRMED; stronger scope OPEN A3187. Under E(R)~LR^beta and a finite logarithmic gradient coefficient M, the negative part after spherical averaging has normalized limsup at most min(M,4(M-H_beta)). This holds for every finite excess M-H_beta, with no smallness restriction. The positive spherical-source coefficient is at most min(M,(2-beta)[1-beta+beta u(M)]+4(M-H_beta)), where u is the confirmed upper tangential envelope. The proof sums a modal quadratic inequality over the complete angular expansion and uses only velocity end-strip bounds. Full Euler applications retain the additional finite-gradient premise; the constant4 is not claimed optimal and no pointwise negative-source bound follows.

LIT390: PROVED at A3181-A3182 CONFIRMED; stronger scope OPEN A3183. A smooth divergence-free field with E_a(R)=LR^beta+O_a(R^-1) and every derivative decaying has gradient coefficient H+delta and a strictly positive negative-spherical-source coefficient kappa>=delta/[4(2-beta)], for every sufficiently small delta>0. An increasing radial phase perturbs the homogeneous leading velocity by a vanishing relative error while its scaled derivative keeps oscillating. The positive spherical-source coefficient is C_s+kappa. The exact whole-space energy correction, all-center estimates and Poisson pressure hold; a nonzero core momentum curl excludes Euler for every c,omega. These controls rule out o(delta) bounds for the negative spherical moment and the upward change in its positive counterpart, without asserting an optimal constant or an Euler counterexample.

LIT389: PROVED at A3188-A3189 CONFIRMED; stronger scope OPEN A3190. Near the least logarithmic gradient coefficient, the positive part after spherical averaging has an O_beta(delta) stability bound and its negative part is O_beta(delta). A normalized degree-one/degree-three perturbation gives opposite nonzero square-root changes in the pointwise positive and negative source moments at the same gradient excess delta. Its spherical-source change is exactly linear in delta. Smooth exact-energy completions prove these orders cannot be improved in the kinematic class; all have Poisson pressure and nonzero full momentum curl for every c,omega. The finite-gradient premise remains additional.

LIT388: CONFIRMED A3173; raw comparison and uniform pointwise constant A3174; broader scope OPEN A3175. Every finite pair (M,tau) between the confirmed lower and upper tangential envelopes is attained with exact gradient coefficient M by a smooth exact-energy completion, including tau=1 above the first toroidal cost. The spherical-source coefficient is simultaneously beta L(2-beta)(1-beta+beta tau). A uniform all-degree identity also bounds the logarithmic energy fraction in degrees ell>=ell0 by min(1,(M-H)/(M_ell0-H)); that bound has exact-cost attaining controls. The inequalities cover every angular mode. All controls admit smooth Poisson pressure and fail full Euler for every c,omega. Neither a pointwise modal exclusion nor a full-profile optimum is claimed.

LIT387: CONFIRMED A3166-A3167; broader scope OPEN A3168. Within the complete six-parameter degree-one homogeneous solenoidal family, exterior q>=0 forces a sharp interval of poloidal energy fractions. The integrated gradient is minimized at one endpoint and the peak gradient at the other; both equality cases force parallel axes. A direction-independent gradient norm in this family necessarily has negative source directions. Smooth exact-energy completions attain the two separate extrema, admit Poisson pressure, and fail full Euler for every c,omega even when the common exterior axis differs from the prescribed rotation axis. These are exact family-restricted countertests, not full-mode or actual-profile optima.

LIT386: CONFIRMED A3161; center invariance A3162; stronger scope OPEN A3163. Under E(R)~LR^beta and limsup G/(beta L log R)<=H_beta+delta, the positive and negative pointwise source moments and the positive spherical-source moment stay within an explicit O(sqrt(delta/beta)) error of their least-gradient values. In particular delta<=beta/10000 forces a negative-source coefficient at least beta L/16. A vanishing negative-source logarithmic liminf forces a strictly larger gradient coefficient, given by an exact scalar root. Two cutoff families handle the gradient limsup without a derivative strip assumption. Full Euler applications retain the additional gradient or source premises; neither premise is derived from velocity decay here.

LIT385: PROVED at A3179 CONFIRMED; stronger scope OPEN A3180. The energy ratio E(R)~LR^beta alone forces a uniform explicit derivative-amplitude gap: D_inf^2>beta L[H_beta+beta/10000]/(4pi). A stronger lower bound is defined by a unique scalar root. The full spherical Hardy gap controls the omitted derivative matrix by10/beta and the missing degree-one energy by1/beta; a fixed-area cap estimate then excludes every amplitude in the stated interval. No minimum-gradient endpoint assumption is used. The full Euler reading retains all energy-theorem hypotheses and does not prove an upper derivative bound or unrestricted nonexistence.

LIT384: CONFIRMED A3157-A3158; broader scope OPEN A3159. A smooth divergence-free exact-energy family has strictly positive quadratic source in every exterior direction for an explicit interval of parameters, with one choice giving an exactly radial exterior source. A separate choice has a scaled Frobenius gradient norm independent of direction and strictly below the stronger minimum-gradient endpoint amplitude. The core completion preserves E_0(R)=LR^beta and all-center O(R^(beta-2)) remainders, admits smooth Poisson pressure, and fails full Euler for every c,omega. These controls reject only kinematic or Poisson-plus-energy inferences; they do not refute the main conjecture or its conditional endpoint bounds.

LIT383: CONFIRMED A3152, with C refinements A3153-A3154; stronger scope A3155 remains OPEN. The exact lower tangential envelope is the piecewise affine interpolation through (M_j,x_j), attained together with the spherical-source bound by smooth completed adjacent-degree controls. The audited companion proof also gives the sharp upper tangential envelope min(1,x_1+(M-H_beta)/(2beta)); for M above t^2+2 its attaining toroidal field has gradient coefficient at most M. The controls fail full Euler for every c,omega. The pointwise-source optimum and automatic finite gradient control remain open. The mixed degree-one/degree-two center error can have exact order R^(beta-1), so it cannot generally be improved by parity.

LIT382: CONFIRMED A3149-A3150; the stronger scope in A3151 remains OPEN. At the minimum logarithmic gradient coefficient H_beta, the full spherical-mode gap fixes the tangential fraction, both pointwise source signs and the positive spherical-source coefficient. It gives the sharper endpoint amplitude D_*, attained by a smooth non-Euler control. The energy ratio alone forces D>D_H, without an automatic endpoint or an optimal general amplitude. All constants and the additional endpoint premise are stated in the standalone proof; no unrestricted Euler nonexistence follows.

LIT381: CONFIRMED A3146; analytical comparison refinements A3147; broader scope OPEN A3148. For C1 divergence-free fields with E(R)~LR^beta, a finite critical gradient coefficient M forces tangential fraction at least 2k^2/[A+sqrt(A^2-8beta k^2)], where k=(beta+1)/2 and A=M+4beta-2. Smooth exact-energy controls attain the tangential and spherical-source bounds at each angular-degree coefficient, admit Poisson pressure, and fail full Euler for every c,omega. Nonzonal equality controls exist, and the root strictly improves both earlier bounds. Intermediate-M and pointwise-source optimality, automatic gradient control and unrestricted nonexistence are not established by this result.

LIT380: CONFIRMED A3139; analytical comparison refinements A3140; full-Euler scope OPEN A3141. One smooth odd divergence-free field has exact E_0(R)=LR^beta after radius2, all-center remainder O(R^(beta-2+epsilon)), and every derivative tending to zero. Its pointwise and spherical positive-source moments attain beta(2-beta)(1-beta)L at every fixed center, with zero limiting tangential fraction. Its weighted gradient moment grows faster than log R but slower than every positive power. Smooth Poisson pressure exists, but the origin momentum curl excludes full Euler for every c,omega. This settles kinematic single-field attainment, including tangential fraction zero, without settling the main conjecture.

LIT379: CONFIRMED A3132; radial refinement and qualified analytical large-gradient sharpness A3133. For C1 divergence-free U with E(R)~LR^beta, a finite critical logarithmic gradient coefficient forces a strict positive-source surplus. The improvement d_beta(M-t^2) and first-order tangential/spherical-source sharpness are separate audited conclusions. LIT378: CONFIRMED A3125-A3126; wider scope OPEN A3127. For C1 divergence-free U with E(R)=LR^beta+O(R^(beta-1)),0<beta<1, the critical squared-gradient moment has lower bound beta H_beta L log R-C at every fixed center, where H_beta=t^2(k^2+4)/(k^2+2), t=(beta-3)/2 and k=(beta+1)/2. A localized spherical-harmonic proof handles the infinite weighted norm. One smooth exact-energy field attains this coefficient, has a smooth Poisson pressure, and fails full Euler for every c,omega. H_beta is an existing Hardy-Leray constant, not a new constant or an optimal full-Euler coefficient.

LIT377: CONFIRMED A3118-A3119; wider scope OPEN A3120. The coefficient beta(2-beta)(1-beta)L in the critical logarithmic moment of the pointwise positive source q_+ is an optimal infimum in the smooth divergence-free class with precise energy growth. A periodic scalar oscillator makes negative angular source mass negligible, then an axisymmetric high-frequency construction and exact core compensation preserve all-center energy and uniform decay. A smooth Poisson pressure exists, but the core momentum curl is nonzero for every c and omega. This is not optimality or a counterexample in the full Euler class.

LIT376: CONFIRMED A3121-A3122; wider scope OPEN A3124. For the positive part Q_+ of the spherical integral of q, the critical logarithmic bound improves by the weighted tangential energy: H_Q>=beta(2-beta)(1-beta)L log R+beta(2-beta)F_tan-C under the precise natural energy law. A smooth exact-energy poloidal family makes the universal coefficient an optimal infimum for H_Q. Every field has a smooth Poisson pressure and fails full Euler for every c and omega. This spherical construction does not decide the pointwise positive-part question; LIT377 covers that distinct kinematic question. Neither settles the actual-profile problem.

LIT375: CONFIRMED A3101-A3102; unrestricted norm membership OPEN A3103. For a continuous field with E(R)=LR^beta+O(R^(beta-1)),0<beta<1, a local alpha-Holder bound,0<alpha<=1, growing at most as R^kappa, with0<=kappa<alpha(1-beta)/3, gives |V|<=C R^(-sigma) and Lp for p>2+beta/sigma, where sigma=[alpha(1-beta)-3kappa]/(2alpha+3). No initial boundedness or divergence condition is needed. Exact-energy smooth divergence-free controls attain the strong threshold and show that even little-o critical seminorm growth permits no finite strong or weak Lp. Their all-center energy law and smooth Poisson pressure hold, but full Euler fails for every c and omega. This proves no unrestricted full-profile integrability or nonexistence claim. The independent reconstruction also proves weak L^(p_star), p_star=2+beta/sigma, with no finite-q Lorentz endpoint improvement. For the stronger weak L^(3c/(1-c)) conclusion, assume2/5<c<1/2, every finite omega, a full profile with bounded C2 velocity and C1 pressure, Hb and DU=o(sqrt(r)). Those derivative-growth hypotheses are additional.

LIT374: CONFIRMED A3098-A3099; automatic critical integrability and unrestricted nonexistence OPEN A3100. If E(R)~LR^beta with0<beta<1, then the positive-source moment of order2-beta has liminf divided by log R at least beta(2-beta)(1-beta)L. Under the precise O(R^(beta-1)) energy remainder the same coefficient gives a lower bound with a finite additive constant, at every fixed center. A homogeneous-power Hessian outside a fixed inner ball leaves only a bounded annular error. The conditional full-Euler weight is2/c-3 for2/5<c<1/2 and every omega. LIT373: CONFIRMED A3094-A3095; automatic integrability OPEN A3097. Natural energy E(R)~L R^beta, 0<beta<1, forces the positive quadratic source moment to grow at least as R^beta about every fixed center. Its critical weighted moment with weight (1+|y-a|)^(2-beta) diverges at least logarithmically. Thus finite weighted q_+ with exponent 2/c-3 conditionally excludes target full Euler profiles for every omega. An exact-energy smooth decaying azimuthal control shows kinematic sharpness of the weight, but fails full momentum for every c,omega. No automatic integrability or unrestricted exclusion is asserted.

LIT372: CONFIRMED A3090-A3091; broader scope OPEN A3092. For every0<beta<1 and L>0, a smooth uniformly decaying divergence-free field has E_a(R)=LR^beta+O_a(R^(beta-1)/log R) about every fixed center and a smooth Poisson pressure, yet belongs to no finite velocity Lp or weak Lp space. Shrinking axis-centered poloidal bumps and exact later azimuthal compensation give the construction. Untouched shells fail full Euler for every c and omega. The countertest blocks only a kinematic or Poisson-plus-energy inference, not the remaining full-PDE implication.

LIT371: CONFIRMED A3079, analytical comparison and flux refinements A3080; automatic work sign and unrestricted scope OPEN A3081. The actual Chae2013 forced-Euler theorem requires one-signed work, strictness on nonzero velocity and q>3N/(N-1) velocity-pressure integrability. Separately, the precise C1 energy law A3053 forces the work U.grad(P+|U|^2/2) to have both signs for a nonzero bounded-Hb target profile, for every omega. No one-sign premise is derived from decay here, and no unrestricted nonexistence follows.

LIT370: CONFIRMED A3087-A3088; broader scope OPEN A3089. The exact target energy asymptotic forces A(r)=rN(r)-E(r) to be negative by a fixed fraction of E on a positive logarithmic measure of radii in every distant multiplicative annulus. A smooth uniformly decaying divergence-free shell construction has that same energy asymptotic but A tending to plus infinity along a sequence and minus infinity along another. It fails full Euler for every c and omega, so only the kinematic upgrade from a lower envelope to a pointwise limit is refuted.

LIT369: CONFIRMED A3072, analytical comparison A3073; automatic exterior signs and unrestricted scope OPEN A3074. For any C1 divergence-free U with E=o(R), an eventually nonpositive spherical mean of q=tr((DU)^2) about any fixed center forces U to vanish outside that inner ball. A separate complete C1 circulation and invariant-volume argument excludes compact full profiles for every finite c!=0 and omega. With confirmed A052-A053/A3053, nonzero bounded-Hb profiles with0<c<1/2 therefore require positive spherical source values arbitrarily far out about every center, including at c=2/5. The sign condition remains additional.

LIT368: CONFIRMED A3084-A3085; broader scope OPEN A3086. A smooth bounded divergence-free field with all derivatives bounded has E(R)=O(R^(log3/log10))=o(R), infinite total energy and nonzero q whose translates return exactly on every compact along10^k e3. It fails uniform decay and, by a direct curl test, the full Euler equation for every c!=0 and omega. Thus sparse recurrence is a kinematic obstruction only. Conversely, for a C1 divergence-free field with E=o(R), source return q(.+na)->q in distributions along a positive-upper-density set of positive integers n, with one fixed nonzero vector a, forces zero without Euler; a nonzero translation period of the source is sufficient.

LIT367: CONFIRMED A3063, analytical comparison A3064; unrestricted scope OPEN A3065. For C1 divergence-free U with E=o(r), the exact identity integral_0^r s integral_(B_s) tr((DU)^2) ds = r integral_(partial B_r)|U.er|^2-E(r) shows that an eventual finite lower bound forces finite energy. Under the target full-profile hypotheses, A052 then gives zero. In particular, an eventually nonincreasing spherical mean of the actual pressure is excluded for a nonzero target profile. LIT366: CONFIRMED A3075, analytical comparison A3076; automatic full-PDE moment premise and unrestricted critical scope OPEN A3077. For a C1 divergence-free field, E(R)=o(R) and a finite positive quadratic moment of q=tr((DU)^2) force finite energy and the exact signed moment identity. LIT365: CONFIRMED A3069, qualified analytical comparison A3070; full construction proofs and unrestricted scope OPEN A3071. Source screen of six actual versioned Euler manuscripts. Current assessment, 2026-11-04T06:01:40.723438+00:00: **no genuine counterexample to the target conjecture has been established**. The target requires a whole-space C2 divergence-free real velocity, uniform decay to zero and the complete rotating Euler profile equation for 2/5<c_l<1/2 and arbitrary finite omega. The conjecture remains OPEN; failure to construct an example is not a proof.

Read the [candidate ledger](#candidate-ledger) for the equation, regularity and decay checks, then [the tested implications](#inequalities-and-dynamical-inferences-tested). The standalone arguments and designated verdicts are indexed by topic and in the complete proof index. Dated updates below are historical records; later verdicts supersede their pending labels.

The principal distinctions are these:

- Exact affine and entire harmonic profiles supply local dynamical countertests but fail decay. Decaying harmonic exterior profiles are singular and have no smooth whole-space extension supplied.
- Steady, translating and rotating steady Euler states cannot also be nonzero decaying similarity profiles under the explicit second-equation hypotheses of A022. This is a restricted class, not a classification of all profiles.
- Kinematic decaying fields disprove implications from decay to finite energy, derivative decay or pressure decay. Their nonzero full-equation residual prevents them from refuting Euler nonexistence.
- Bracket models can satisfy divergence, recurrence and surface identities with an independent W. Equality W=curl U is essential; the named model reconstructions have their own confirmed obstructions.
- LIT352, CONFIRMED A2952-A2954, gives smooth flat two-complex-component counterexamples above the logarithmic coefficient1/8. The positive side is LIT351, CONFIRMED A2942. Neither is an Euler field or a counterexample for a single complex scalar.
- LIT353, CONFIRMED A2963-A2964, defeats summation of separate upper-limit weak tails and finite-exceptional-set localization from the coefficient bound alone. It leaves lower-limit zero-index charge budgets intact and constructs no solution with that coefficient.
- LIT354, CONFIRMED A2976-A2977, retains its distributional equation, all-order integral flatness and exact radial majorant. General weak-Ld, higher-dimensional logarithmic sharpness and Euler coefficient implications remain OPEN A2979. LIT354 alone supplies no sharpness construction; the inverse-radius question is separately addressed by LIT356 and LIT357 below.
- LIT356 is CONFIRMED A2999-A3000: smooth flat finite-vector counterexamples for every C>1/4 and every complex source dimension, with the ambient radius and target dimension depending on k,C. This supplies no scalar, fixed-target or Euler counterexample.
- LIT355 is CONFIRMED A2991-A2992; broader implications remain OPEN A2994. Its exact switching ratio and vector-amplitude asymptotic concern only the auxiliary LIT352 construction.

LIT357 is CONFIRMED A3005: the same ambient bound for every C>1/4 with fixed target dimension2k, hence any prescribed N>=2k, using a different envelope. Its analytical comparison has the diagnostic qualifications of A3006. Smaller targets, higher-dimensional logarithmic sharpness, scalar failure and Euler consequences remain OPEN A3007.

The Pan-Zhang sequel source audit now includes full readings of both twelve-page versions and seven rendered publisher pages. Its reductions and comparison are CONFIRMED A2983-A2984/A2986, the peripheral universal sentence REFUTED A2985, the literal n=2 source condition confirmed A2990 and broader scope OPEN A2987. The rigid-state exclusion is written independently in the team records. It covers a full non-axisymmetric class and every c>0,omega, but assumes a second rigid Euler equation in addition to the profile equation. It does not classify general rotating similarity profiles.

## Inequalities and dynamical inferences tested

**Rotating Bernoulli sign.** The exact constant profile b=(-c,omega/2,0) gives (cy+U)·B=-omega²/4 at y=e_1. Thus sign-definite Bernoulli transport cannot be asserted on the uniformly sublinear class. This particular example has nonzero constant far-field velocity. For a decaying field the sign becomes positive sufficiently far away, but that does not control recurrent trajectories inside the bounded core.

**Positive divergence.** Positive div B does not exclude periodic B-orbits. The exact affine circular-core profile also has every backward orbit bounded. It fails sublinearity. The independent auditor additionally constructed a smooth compactly supported divergence-free perturbation of the similarity drift with a periodic circle; that example satisfies velocity decay but is only kinematic, not an exact Euler profile. These examples expose different missing premises and are not interchangeable.

**Strain and equilibrium spectra.** At omega=2, U=(y_1,-y_2,0) is an exact affine profile with max eigenvalue(sym DU)=1 and spec DB={c,c±i√3}. A strain threshold therefore does not force a negative-real-part eigenvalue of DB in rotating coordinates. Convergent backward basins can instead be treated using the transported two-form and an adapted norm.

**Velocity decay and energy.** The smooth divergence-free field (1+|y|²)^{-(alpha+1)/2}Jy decays for alpha>0 and has infinite energy for alpha≤3/2. Its energy growth at alpha=1/c-1 is exactly the power 5-2/c expected in the campaign interval. It is not an exact profile. No inference from U→0 to L² is justified by function-space reasoning alone.

**Velocity decay and derivative decay.** The smooth divergence-free field sin(|y|⁴)Jy/(1+|y|²) tends to zero, but its derivative is unbounded along an explicit sequence. Any proof obtaining DU→0 must use the PDE or add that condition.

**An inviscid version of the positive adjoint weight.** Under c>0 and sublinearity, every C^1 solution of div(Bw)=0 vanishes because its value grows exponentially along a bounded backward trajectory unless it is zero. Thus the positive smooth Gaussian weight in Pineau-Vicol's viscous argument has no counterpart obtained by simply deleting the Laplacian. The complete proof is in the team records.

**Sampling a periodic limit orbit.** Approach to a periodic orbit as a set does not ensure convergence to a periodic point of one fixed time map. A separate continuous-flow proof can remove this arithmetic issue under a point-or-cycle classification of almost every alpha-limit set. The conditional theorem is in the team records, CONFIRMED as A020. The point-or-cycle classification remains an additional assumption.

**Pressure decay.** Let `R=|y|` and `U=Jy/sqrt((1+R^2)log(e+R^2))`. This smooth divergence-free velocity and its derivative tend to zero, but every smooth solution of its pressure Poisson equation has spherical mean

\[
 \overline P(R)=\tfrac13\log\log(e+R^2)+C+o(1).
\]

The proof averages the exact Poisson source over spheres and integrates the resulting radial equation. Entire harmonic corrections have constant spherical mean and cannot remove the divergence. This velocity fails the azimuthal component of the full profile equation. Thus it tests a pressure inference, not the main conjecture or the canonical-gradient theorem. The complete proof, CONFIRMED as A039, is in the team records.

**Period of a closed similarity trajectory.** When `c!=1/2`, the circulation identity excludes nonconstant periodic `B` orbits if `omega=0`; for nonzero `omega`, it and the periodic Wirtinger inequality give `T>=2pi/abs(omega)` for every such orbit. Equality forces a horizontal circular orbit with angular speed `-omega`. The exact affine circular-core field realizes equality but fails decay. For nonzero rotation this sharp restriction alone excludes neither all periodic orbits nor all nonperiodic recurrent structure; it is CONFIRMED as A030, with proof in the team records.

**Angular exterior profiles.** Every harmonic potential produces an exact rotating profile with an explicit pressure. The dipole `phi=y_1/|y|^3` gives a non-axisymmetric velocity decaying cubically for every `c,omega`, but singular at the origin. This refutes an exterior angular-mode exclusion based on rotation alone. Harmonic Liouville excludes an entire smooth decaying extension within the potential class. The direct identity is in the team records, CONFIRMED as A038.

## Joint frozen-vorticity restrictions do not yet give nonexistence

The complete frozen-vorticity flow is now established without a global bound on DU. Under uniform sublinearity its trajectories from a compact initial set grow at most as `(1+|t|)^((c+epsilon)/(1+c))`; bounded U permits epsilon zero. For `0<c<1/2`, volume preservation then forces almost-everywhere recurrence in both time directions. These conclusions are CONFIRMED as A048-A049. They concern the autonomous field Omega, not the similarity field B, and do not imply that every Omega orbit is bounded.

A smooth decaying kinematic field shows the limits of this information. On a compact toroidal shell, start with the one-form `A=g(r)(dphi-sqrt(2)dtheta)` and take its curl. An explicit angular change of variables conjugates each regular vorticity orbit to an irrational linear flow on a torus. A Newtonian gradient correction makes the velocity divergence-free while preserving its curl. The resulting nonzero field is smooth and decays, its vorticity is compactly supported, every frozen orbit is bounded and recurrent, there is no nonconstant closed frozen orbit, its frozen derivative grows at most polynomially, its derivative at each vorticity zero is nilpotent, and every compact invariant measure has zero mean of U dot Omega.

The construction and all these properties are CONFIRMED as A051 in the team records. It fails the full profile equation for every c>0 and omega, as follows from A029. Thus even the combined frozen-flow tests do not by themselves settle the PDE. Its invariant tori belong to Omega. By contrast, positive divergence excludes compact embedded closed invariant surfaces of B, including B-invariant tori, as CONFIRMED in A050. Confusing these two flows would produce a false contradiction.

## Entropy and transported-field counterexamples

A smooth three-dimensional constant-roof suspension of the hyperbolic toral map with matrix [[2,1],[1,1]] has one invariant time-one probability on each phase fiber. Each is ergodic, has entropy log((3+sqrt(5))/2), satisfies the entropy formula, and has absolutely continuous unstable conditional measures. Its time-one statistical basin nevertheless has zero ambient volume, since time-one dynamics preserves the phase coordinate. These probabilities are not invariant under the full flow. The construction is CONFIRMED as A064 in the team records. It refutes the shortcut from the time-one entropy formula to physicality, and makes no claim against a theorem that additionally uses full-flow invariance and a basin argument.

A065 constructs a flow-ergodic maximizing probability for a hypothetical smooth profile. If it sees nonzero vorticity, its entropy and Lyapunov exponents give unstable absolute continuity. It may instead be supported on Omega=0. These alternatives must be distinguished; neither positive entropy nor existence of a maximizer gives a contradiction by itself.

The coordinator's decoupled construction is independently CONFIRMED as A066, with its global smooth extension CONFIRMED as A121. It supplies smooth compactly supported divergence-free U and a nonzero globally smooth divergence-free field W obeying [B,W]=-(1+c)W for every c in (0,1/2) and finite omega. The extended W is flat along two exceptional proper lines. A compact trapping handlebody carries recurrent dynamics, but its stated construction contains an equilibrium and does not itself exhibit a nonsingular regular basic attractor. No Euler pressure or equality W=curl U is supplied. Indeed A067 shows that the nonzero set of W has infinite volume, while curl U is compactly supported. This obstructs a proof using the bracket, divergence and smoothness alone. It is not an Euler counterexample.

Arnaud-Fejoz's published conformal symplectic example provides further context: an exact symplectic four-manifold can have a compact three-dimensional Anosov-suspension attractor with entropy equal to the absolute conformal rate. The forms can be checked explicitly, with two minor displayed-formula corrections recorded in the team records. Its dimension, topology and equation differ from the Euler target. It shows why an entropy lower bound from a transported form requires a second obstruction before it can establish nonexistence.

## Constant far-field limits, translations and Galilean transformations

A nonconstant exact profile with U→b is a potentially relevant candidate. Set M=cI-omega J and h=-M^{-1}b. Then

\[
 U^h(y)=U(y+h)+Mh
\]

has zero far-field limit and remains an exact profile after adding the explicit linear pressure correction in the standalone proof. Thus the constant-limit class is equivalent to the decaying class up to this profile transformation, and a nonconstant candidate must be normalized before dismissal. Constant fields themselves become zero. An arbitrary Galilean boost of a physical solution need not retain stationarity in similarity coordinates.

Intrinsic dilation U_λ(y)=λU(y/λ) preserves c,omega and decay. It changes ||U||_∞ by λ while leaving ||DU||_∞ unchanged. This prevents velocity amplitude alone from serving as an independent perturbative parameter without a fixed spatial normalization.

## Source examples and numerical candidates

Two explicit pairs printed in Chae-Shvydkoy arXiv:1201.6009v2, page 5, fail that version's own equation (6) when directly substituted. The parallel shear in (11) gives nonparallel left and right sides; the point vortex in (12) needs a nonzero radial pressure. The corrected vortex remains singular. The PDF rendering was checked to rule out extraction error. These findings concern those printed expressions; the journal version has not yet been compared, and no conclusion about the paper's Liouville proofs follows merely from these example errors.

The existing approach3 and degree-6 experiments contain numerical searches and validation artifacts, not an exact nonzero whole-space solution of the target PDE. Basis constraints, finite samples and a small residual cannot certify existence. No additional optimization search was launched by this agent. Critical c=1/2 profiles, including candidates discussed in the supplied approach1 references, are outside the strict interval of this campaign and must not be presented as counterexamples below one half.

## What a proof still must exclude

The unresolved candidate class is a smooth, decaying, fully three-dimensional rotating profile whose backward dynamics can have nonperiodic recurrent structure and whose slowly decaying velocity may have infinite energy. It cannot be eliminated by assuming axisymmetry, replacing decay by L², importing viscosity, or silently converting a sampled-period criterion into an unconditional trajectory theorem. The current explicit examples show those proof failures without settling existence in the target class.

## Actual compact-support constructions checked

The full Gavrilov manuscript, arXiv:1810.08020v1, has now been read. Its pressure cutoff removes the singular central circle from the support and produces a smooth whole-space steady Euler field. Baldi arXiv:2302.02982v1, Section 1 and Theorem 1.1, supplies actual periodic and quasiperiodic velocity trajectories in pressure tori. These are valid examples against any assertion that smooth decaying steady Euler dynamics must be trivial or consist only of stationary points. They do not satisfy the similarity PDE: A022 rules out the simultaneous equations, independently of omega. See LITERATURE_REVIEW Section 6.1 for exact source scope and the distinction among U, B and Omega trajectories.

LIT19 and LIT20 are CONFIRMED as A089 and A094. Positive-entropy measures supported on vorticity zeros require vanishing jets; real analyticity excludes that alternative. A vortical entropy-saturating full-flow ergodic measure is physical for continuous-time backward averages. Smooth flat zero sets and vortical physical measures have not been excluded for the target class. Neither result produces a counterexample or settles nonexistence.


## Recent columnar singularity claim

Adda-Bedia and Rica, arXiv:2605.17569v2, was read as an actual 21-page manuscript. It does not provide a target counterexample. The separated cylindrical ansatz in equations (3.1)-(3.3) has components r a(r/R)/s, z b(r/R)/s, and z g(r/R)/s. Read globally, every nonzero continuous instance fails spatial decay and has infinite kinetic energy. Restricting it to an inner cylinder leaves a matching problem.

There are also two distinct regularity failures. The proposed positive-radius interface has g proportional to (1-r/R)^(alpha/2), with 1<alpha<2, so even C1 fails there. At the axis, the selected series has g proportional to r^2; its Cartesian azimuthal component contains x|x| along a line and is not C2. Neither issue is repaired by convergence of the numerical radial series.

The proposed direct irrotational outer matching cannot hold continuously across a finite upper cap carrying nonzero swirl: circles just outside have zero circulation by Stokes, whereas the limiting inner circle has circulation 2pi r L g(r/R)/s. A vortical transition region could evade that particular obstruction, but would be additional, unconstructed work. The standalone calculation and exact scope are in the team records, LIT23 CONFIRMED A112.

## Helicity cancellation is a restriction, not a nonexistence proof

LIT24/A114 proves, under H0 C2 and uniform sublinearity for 0<c<1/2 and every omega, that U dot curl U either vanishes identically or changes sign. If this density is absolutely integrable, its total integral is zero. The proof uses the conservative complete vorticity flow and a global primitive of the helicity density. Neither absolute integrability nor identically zero vorticity follows from the argument. The toroidal kinematic example and the coordinator's independent transported-field construction remain reminders that action cancellation must not be mistaken for the missing Euler curl-coupling contradiction.


## Joint tests of energy, helicity, recurrence and far-field compactness

LIT27 and LIT28 are independently CONFIRMED as A132 and A133. Both use a smooth divergence-free field U=A(r)a(r) cross n, where n=y/r, |a|=1, and A=r^(-p) outside a compact core, with p=1/c-1 in (1,3/2). An exact compact core adjustment gives

\[
 E(R)=\frac{8\pi}{3(3-2p)}R^{3-2p},\qquad R\ge1.
\]

Their actual vorticity never vanishes outside the unit ball and has radial component 2A(a dot n)/r. This makes its frozen flow complete and conservative with radius growth at most a constant times (1+|t|)^(c/(1+c)). Thus these tests retain the campaign's frozen-flow recurrence and exterior-density conclusions.

Rapid turning in LIT27 makes DU unbounded and both signed helicity integrals infinite. Slow turning in LIT28 instead gives uniform DU=O(r^-2), Dini derivative decay and absolutely integrable, sign-changing helicity with zero total integral. Its exact cylindrical angular support is {-1,0,1}. In both examples R^p U(Ry) tends weakly to zero on fixed annuli while its L2 norm stays positive. These combined scalar and geometric conditions cannot by themselves justify a nonzero weak tail or strong rescaling. Each example has an explicitly nonzero profile curl residual for every finite omega. Neither challenges a theorem that uses the full momentum equation.

LIT29, CONFIRMED A145 with the residual correction A146, adds a canonical smooth L2 Poisson pressure and a nonzero vertical mean component. A second scalar core adjustment makes the centered-ball angular-momentum balance exact as well. Odd velocity and even pressure make the centered-ball helicity identity exact by parity, while tangency to spheres removes the energy pressure flux. For every R>=1, all three centered-ball energy, angular-momentum and helicity balances hold with the same canonical pressure. Rescaling has a nonzero weak homogeneous limit but loses a fixed positive fraction of its annular energy. Its explicit curl residual still rules out every full profile pressure. These are centered-ball identities, not identities on arbitrary domains or on every shrinking invariant domain. The distinction is essential to A124's pointwise-defect reconstruction.

## Exact local periodic geometry and complex mode screens

Coordinator Example 13.3, independently CONFIRMED A144, solves the complete profile equation on the domain r>0. Put k=1-2c>0, a>0 and kappa!=0. Its cylindrical velocity is

    U_r=-r/2+k a^2/(2r),
    U_theta=kappa(r-a^2/r),
    U_z=z.

The explicit pressure is

    P=(1/8+kappa^2/2)r^2
      -(k^2/2+2kappa^2)a^2 log(r/a)
      -(k^2/8+kappa^2/2)a^4/r^2-z^2.

It has curl U=2kappa e_z. For omega!=0 the circle r=a,z=0 is a regular B orbit of least period 2pi/abs(omega), with zero circulation, retrograde orientation and transverse exponents 2c-1 and 1+c. The exact component check and variational calculation are in [the auditor's standalone proof](auditor_proofs/proofs/local_periodic_saddle/PROOF.md). The cycle is a saddle in each time direction. The field is singular on the axis and unbounded at infinity. It supplies no compact backward attractor and no global counterexample. It does show that these periodic-orbit constraints and the actual Euler curl coupling are locally compatible.

A140 supplies a different screen. With f=sin(r)/r extended analytically at zero, psi=(partial_x+i partial_y)^L f, T=curl(psi y), and Z=curl T+T, one has curl Z=Z. Thus `(Z dot grad)Z=grad(Z dot Z/2)` in the complex bilinear equation. Z is smooth, decays as O(r^-1), and has precisely character L. Its Hermitian energy is `L(L+1) norm_S2((n_x+i n_y)^L)^2 R+O(log R)`. This is a counterexample to exclusion based solely on smoothness, decay and the isolated highest-mode complex steady equation. Its remaining mode equations and natural profile energy law fail. The full construction is in [A140's proof](auditor_proofs/proofs/finite_angular_spectrum/PROOF.md).

The real homogeneous existence and exclusion sources are compared in LITERATURE_REVIEW Section 6.5. They cannot be transferred to a complex top mode by replacing bilinear squares with Hermitian norms. LIT31 records the restricted complex irrotational and constant-direction exclusions and their complete arguments. Neither the sources nor those two subclass arguments classify the general smooth spherical complex steady tail.

## Global first integrals and regular dynamical models

Under C2 profile regularity and uniform sublinearity, globally radial-tangent vorticity about a fixed center forces U to be constant for every c>0. Divergence-free tangential vorticity on every sphere has a Hamiltonian first integral; a nonconstant one has a regular closed level and hence a periodic vortex line, which exact Cauchy transport excludes. At 0<c<1/2, two global real C2 first integrals independent wherever Omega is nonzero also force constancy, by Sard's theorem, vorticity recurrence and the one-dimensional topology of regular common fibers. Neither proper fibers nor compact common levels are assumed. Both conclusions are CONFIRMED A156-A157, with complete statements and proofs in LIT32. They impose global geometric conditions not known to follow from decay. Local Clebsch charts do not satisfy that global hypothesis.

A suspended DA attractor has the right topology for the regular alternative, but this does not establish the required kinematics. The confirmed decoupled model CO6/A066 contains a Lorenz-type singularity. Its smooth extension A121 therefore does not realize a nonsingular regular basic attractor. The actual DA sources supply no constant-cocycle or curl-coupled realization. In particular an untouched periodic orbit of a local DA modification of a volume-preserving toral suspension retains return determinant one. This contradicts the exp(-3cT) required by constant negative divergence after any positive time change or smooth coordinate change, CONFIRMED A170. The claim retains the untouched-orbit hypothesis and permits further derivative alterations. The more general necessary Euler ratio -(1+c)/(1-2c) is CONFIRMED A164. Neither result excludes all DA models.

The direct BBY17 flow source identifies infinite stable gaps with finitely many free periodic weak stable cylinders. They are volume-null and hence do not conflict with almost-everywhere vortex recurrence. Its affine normal-form theorem explicitly excludes attractors and repellers. No normalization of the requested attracting plug follows. Grines-Zhuzhoma's separate global stable-trace compactness inference is false, CONFIRMED A163, while its preceding gap statement is not refuted. Exact sources and limitations are in in the team records.


## Critical-tail inversion preserves a missing nonlinear equation

LIT36, CONFIRMED A179, starts from the smooth independent singular model A121, whose compactly supported velocity U0 and divergence-free field W obey [Ay+U0,W]=-(1+c)W. Its exterior W has exactly the nonvanishing critical weak L^(3c) tail. Its autonomous flow is complete, volume preserving and almost everywhere recurrent. These facts do not make W the curl of U0.

For 2/5<c<1/2 the absolutely convergent Biot-Savart inverse V is smooth on R3, divergence free, tends to zero and has curl V=W. It has a nonzero rotating homogeneous leading term Vh of degree 1-1/c, with V-Vh=O(r^-3) through derivatives and energy C R^(5-2/c)+C_defect+o(1). This exhibits strong rotating rescaling at the natural infinite-energy threshold. It does not satisfy the old carrier equation automatically, since its carrier has changed from Ay+U0 to Ay+V.

The exact remaining equation is [V-U0,W]=0. Outside the core the residual is O(r^(-2/c)); an exact profile would additionally require the leading steady-Euler condition [Vh,curl Vh]=0. LIT39/A195 now proves that every nonzero scalar amplitude of THIS reconstruction has nonzero residual. No universal failure for every independent construction has been proved. This is a precise construction test for the singular kinematic model, not a regular-attractor example or a target counterexample. The standalone argument is in the team records.

## Conditional rigidity tests available to screen a candidate

For a C2 exact uniformly sublinear profile and any 0<c<1/2, finite omega, spatially constant reduced H forces U=0 by LIT51/A256. More generally, grad H=0 on the vortical set forces U spatially constant by LIT55-LIT56/A265-A266; decay then gives U=0. Open regions of constant H covering almost every vortical point suffice, even with initially different constants. These statements use the full Euler equation and should not be applied to the independent-field models above.

For C2 decaying profiles with 2/5<=c<1/2, LIT59-LIT60/A277-A278/A283 gives a stronger basin test. A single normalized H0 has spatial-mean-square decay and exponentially decaying transported L2 norm. On a given vortical SRB essential basin with positive local unstable conditional densities, one sign of helicity forces H0=0. Coverage by these basins gives U=0. This does not require mixing or a dense vortical set. At C2 the basin existence and coverage premises remain open; A308 supplies them for smooth profiles. Exact estimates and proofs are collected in [the literature review](LITERATURE_REVIEW.md), Section 8.3.

The earlier LIT57/A269 rank argument assumes C3, zero helicity, and a dense vortical set. Its potential K_rank with B K_rank=(BH)^2 is different from the asymptotic coefficient K below. Open irrotational regions are not covered by that rank argument. LIT58/A273's mixing-based version is also a conditional result, now superseded in its decay application by the nonmixing basin argument.

The measure construction has a separate audit trail. LIT65-LIT67/A305-A307 now supplies all finite-time curve premises for the smooth application. This change proves smooth coverage and does not validate the discarded measure inequality.

## Almost-everywhere Bernoulli coefficient under the target assumptions

Updated 2026-11-03T00:02:55.398664+00:00. Independently CONFIRMED A287-A289, for every C2 decaying profile with 2/5<=c<1/2 and finite omega. Set a=1-2c, B=(cI-omega J)y+U, q=|U|^2/2, and H0=P0+q+((cI-omega J)y).U using the single fixed canonical pressure P0. There is K in L2loc such that, on one full-volume set invariant under every real carrier time,

    exp(a t) H0(Phi_t y) -> K(y),
    K(Phi_s y)=exp(-a s)K(y),
    K(y)=H0(y)+a int_0^infinity exp(a t)(P0-q)(Phi_t y) dt.

The integral is absolutely convergent. On each bounded Q the L2 norm of the future supremum of the difference is at most C_Q exp(-cT). Thus H0 tends to zero at its endpoint rate exp(-at) along almost every carrier orbit. Complete statements and proofs: in the team records and in the team records. No derivative decay, Dini estimate, helicity sign, invariant measure or basin assumption is used.

K is not known continuous or locally bounded. A compact singular invariant support may lie outside the full-volume domain; its values cannot be inferred from this theorem. A smooth scalar-shell example confirms that the earlier spatial-mean estimate alone did not imply pointwise convergence. LIT63 is independently CONFIRMED A292-A293: K belongs to every local Lp with p<2(1-c)/(1-2c). Exact backward volume contraction forces any covariant scalar to vanish if it instead belongs to a local Lp space with p>=3c/(1-2c), including equality. In the primary range the guaranteed interval falls strictly below this threshold. The radial scalar |y|^(-(1-2c)/c) for the linear carrier shows kinematic sharpness; it is not an actual Euler Bernoulli coefficient. Even proving K=0 would require a separate argument for U=0. No nonexistence conclusion follows here.

## Two positive backward exponents at an actual vortical equilibrium

Updated: 2026-11-03T00:32:22.765123+00:00. The independently confirmed A304 example rules out a local exclusion based only on the actual curl relation, bracket transport and pressure. Let 0<c<1/2, omega!=0, a=1-2c, kappa=1+c, and choose 0<epsilon<a/2 with L0=a-epsilon. Put

    D=diag(-L0-c,-epsilon-c,1),
    U(y)=Dy+omega Jy,
    B(y)=diag(-L0,-epsilon,kappa)y,
    Omega=2omega e3.

Then div U=0 and [B,Omega]=-kappa Omega. The exact pressure is P(y)=-y.Sy/2, where

    S=D(I+D)+omega J(I+2D)+omega^2 J^2.

The matrix S is symmetric because the first two diagonal entries of D sum to -1. Direct substitution gives the full profile equation. The equilibrium atom at zero has backward exponents (L0,epsilon,-kappa) and entropy zero. For any fixed r, taking epsilon=a/(2(r+1)) makes epsilon-(L0-epsilon)/(r-1)<0, so that particular finite-r lower estimate does not exclude the atom. The complete proof [A304](auditor_proofs/proofs/two_expanding_equilibrium/PROOF.md) was read. The velocity is unbounded. No spatial-average estimate for that nonzero F can decay faster than exp(-3ct), regardless of a higher integrability power.
